# Step 6 — optimising and repairing the existing renderer paths

Generated by `scratch/step6/report.py` from `examples/results/step6/`, from the frozen Step 3
labels and from the frozen Step 4 certification. Every number and every count on this page is
computed from one of those files and names it; nothing here is maintained by hand. Where the
coordinator's dashboard and a committed artifact disagree the page prints the **artifact**, and
the disagreement is listed under *Where the dashboard and the artifacts disagree*.

## Scope: which cells owe correctness, and which owe throughput

These are not the same set, and Step 6 spent a week reporting them as if they were.

Step 2 §4.2 — `docs/research/certification.md`: **The configuration being certified must be the registered SWM default.**

Step 2 §12.2 puts completion on that same default — `docs/research/certification.md`:

```text
registered-default render coverage == full
BACKEND_CERTIFIED(cpu)  AND  BACKEND_CERTIFIED(gpu)
i.e.  CPU tier >= CPU_FAST   AND   GPU tier >= GPU_FAST
```

So the accounting splits, and the page splits with it:

| accounting                                                        | denominator                            | why that denominator                                                                                                                    |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| correctness and fidelity — Gate A, replay identity, digests       | all 49 render-distinct cells           | a parameter-space cell exists to prove the renderer answers the whole parameter space; getting it wrong is a defect wherever it happens |
| performance completion — CPU_FAST+, GPU_FAST+, remaining blockers | the 36 `registered_default` cells only | §4.2 and §12.2 put the certified configuration and `ENVIRONMENT_DONE` on the registered SWM default; no other cell is owed a tier       |

The bar itself is read from the frozen spec, not restated here — `scratch/certification.json`, `ladder.environment_done_min_tiers`: **CPU >= `CPU_FAST`**, **GPU >= `GPU_FAST`**.

## Correctness after Step 6 — all 49 frozen configurations

- **Gate A** is the Step 4 status, *overridden only where a Step 6 certification artifact
  states a status for that configuration*. Where two Step 6 arms disagree — the
  texture-fidelity 3.11 and 3.12 reference pairs are two arms of one diagnosis — the arm that
  ran under the declared reference, the one whose certifier blocked nothing, is the status;
  both arms are printed in the detail column and the disagreement is never hidden.
- **replay identity** counts the committed before/after pixel and digest comparisons that
  mention the cell. `identical xN` means N comparisons found no moved pixel; a cell with no
  comparison says so rather than being credited with one.

### Superseded certification records

A workstream that re-runs a certification commits the new record beside the old one. Both are
evidence; only one is the verdict. Among records for the same configuration **and the same
declared reference version**, the current record is the one whose stamped `git_commit` is
newest by ancestry on the tree reported above (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`; committer date
then hash where two commits are on branches that never saw each other; file mtime only where
a record is unstamped). The rest are listed here and were used for nothing:

## Performance after Step 6 — the 36 registered/default configurations

The cells §4.2 and §12.2 put the contract on. **CPU tier / GPU tier** are the representative
Step 3 labels, *overridden only where a Step 6 artifact re-measures that row*; a re-measured
tier is read from a tier artifact or computed from the raw shard by `scratch/step3/certify.py`,
Step 3's own evaluator against Step 2's frozen ladder, never a second implementation of it.
`missing` is an absence of evidence — no measurement exists — and is counted apart from a
measurement that came out below the bar.

| config                   | CPU tier        | src | GPU tier        | src | meets the §12.2 bar |
|--------------------------|-----------------|-----|-----------------|-----|---------------------|
| `Acrobot`                | CPU_REGRESSION  | s3  | GPU_FASTER      | s4  | no                  |
| `CartPole`               | CPU_PARITY      | s3  | GPU_FASTER      | s4  | no                  |
| `CraftaxClassic`         | CPU_EXCEPTIONAL | s7  | GPU_EXCEPTIONAL | s8  | no                  |
| `DMCAcrobotSwingup`      | CPU_FASTER      | s10 | GPU_FAST        | s11 | no                  |
| `DMCBallInCupCatch`      | CPU_FASTER      | s10 | GPU_FAST        | s13 | no                  |
| `DMCCartpoleBalance`     | CPU_PARITY      | s11 | GPU_FAST        | s11 | no                  |
| `DMCCartpoleSwingup`     | CPU_FASTER      | s10 | GPU_FAST        | s11 | no                  |
| `DMCCheetahRun`          | CPU_FASTER      | s16 | GPU_EXCEPTIONAL | s11 | no                  |
| `DMCFingerTurnHard`      | CPU_REGRESSION  | s11 | GPU_FAST        | s11 | no                  |
| `DMCHopperHop`           | CPU_FASTER      | s16 | GPU_EXCEPTIONAL | s11 | no                  |

## Render-parameter-space configurations — the other 13

These cells are certified for **correctness**: they exist to show that the renderer answers
the whole declared parameter space, not only the registered default. The frozen contract asks
them for no tier, so a tier measured here is **reported, not owed**, and none of them is ever
counted as a performance blocker. Reporting them as blockers — which this page did until
Sami's 2026-09-15 decision — inflates the remaining work with an obligation nobody wrote down.

A blocker on this list is a cell that has not reached the bar. It is **not** a statement that
nothing further can be done for it: the work that was identified and then not funded is listed
under *Deferred possible work*, and the cases where an attempt was made and the ceiling was
measured are under *Demonstrated blockers*. Reading this table without those two sections
turns "not done" into "impossible", which is the one conclusion the evidence does not
support.

Step-4 replay legs this workstream committed, with the pass/fail each artifact records
for itself. `pass` is measured against the FROZEN oracle, which for most of the corpus
was taken on CUDA — so a CPU leg failing everywhere is a statement about which device
the oracle was frozen on, not about the patch. What a patch has to show is that its
after leg is no worse than its own baseline leg, and that comparison is made below.

## Decisions taken, decisions pending

Decisions already taken and recorded, verbatim:

| date       | by          | decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|------------|-------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 2026-09-14 | Sami        | CPU 3-D backend work APPROVED. Policy: backend implementation may differ by device, renderer semantics may not; CPU backend certified against the same frozen contract; no MJWarp-on-CPU for symmetry; one shared CPU-native backend for the MJWarp-only scenes (Fetch if feasible), no bespoke per-env approximations; device="cpu" must be a genuine CPU backend, never silent CUDA fallback; minimize coupling toward a later Scene3D extraction without blocking Step 6; Fetch CPU feasibility to be quantified before a large implementation. |
| 2026-09-15 | coordinator | Certification partition cannot provide a sole-tenant CPU node under the normal QOS (cpu=64 cap vs whole-node exclusive charge); 1-thread cores_cpu shards with a same-node decode bar are not obtainable there for gfxdraw. S_cpu rows stand; cores_cpu left unasserted rather than borrowed. Needs a QOS with a node-sized CPU cap or free 48-core nodes (escalate to Sami / Step 8).                                                                                                                                                             |

## Project semantic status

Step 5's parity-tier semantic gate PASSED, and Step 6 was not permitted to invalidate it. The
condition for carrying it forward is mechanical — no workstream whose code is on this branch
may have changed representative pixels or owe a semantic follow-up — so it is asserted here
rather than described. *Merged* below is decided by **a merge commit on this branch** — not by a status string, because `raster2d` calls itself partially merged and `raycast3d` calls itself still in implementation while the code of both is in the tree this page describes.

**All 7 workstreams with code on this branch record `pixels_changed: no`**
**and `semantic_followup: none`.** No merged Step-6 change moved a representative pixel, so
nothing reaches the surfaces Step 5 measured and the Step-5 parity-tier gate stands exactly
as Step 5 left it. That is a statement about the merged set only: the held and unmerged commits
listed above have not been through this check, and the pixel comparisons each workstream

23 artifact(s) were **withdrawn by the workstream that took them** — moved into a
